\\\"\\\"\\\"Extract entropy + tail_decay (slope formula) for all texts.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    results = []\\n    for start in range(0, len(texts), batch_size):\\n        batch_texts = texts[start:start + batch_size]\\n        encodings = tokenizer(\\n            batch_texts, return_tensors=\\\"pt\\\", padding=True,\\n            truncation=True, max_length=512,\\n        ).to(device)\\n\\n        with 1, :]\\n            log_probs = torch.log_softmax(token_logits, dim=-1)\\n            probs = torch.exp(log_probs)\\n            entropy = -(probs * log_probs).sum(dim=-1)\\n            entropy_np = entropy.float().cpu().numpy()\\n\\n            # tail_decay: slope of sorted avg log-probs (ranks 100-1000)\\n            avg_log_probs = log_probs.float().mean(dim=0)\\n            sorted_lp, _ = np.polyfit(x, y, 1)\\n                tail_decay = float(coeffs[0])\\n            else:\\n                tail_decay = 0.0\\n\\n            results.append({\\n                \\\"entropy_mean\\\": float(np.mean(entropy_np)),\\n                \\\"entropy_std\\\": float(np.std(entropy_np)),\\n                \\\"entropy_min\\\": float(np.min(entropy_np)),\\n                \\\"entropy_max\\\": float(np.max(entropy_np)),\\n                \\\"tail_decay\\\": tail_decay,\\n            })\\n\\n        processed = min(start + batch_size, len(texts))\\n        if (start // batch_size) % 50 == Critical findings so far:

   - 7-feature binary F1 = 0.6944 (this is the 0.694 in Table 3)
   - This is a **pooled LOGO** F1 (n_samples = 29,095 for binary)
   - Uses XGB 200/6
   
2. **`8gen_baselines.json`** (from `eval_8gen_baselines_pooled.py`):
   - 7feature_ours shows a pooled binary F1 of 0.6935, which is slightly lower than the 0.6944 I found earlier
   - The 4det_surface baseline actually achieves 0.7044, exceeding the reported 0.694
   - 4det_ensemble comes in at 0.6993

3. **Llama/Mistral data**: I notice these models have feature files but are missing the corresponding detection score files, so I need to investigate what's actually in those feature files.

4. **Key discrepancy**: There's a mismatch I'm trying to pin down.

The `eval_8gen_baselines_pooled.py` script uses different hyperparameters for different models—`XGB_BASE` (100/3) for 4det_surface versus `XGB_OURS` (200/6) for the 7-feature approach. The 4det_surface model uses 6 features including average log probability, fastdetectgpt, binoculars, ld_score, type-token ratio, and hapax ratio.

What's striking is that 4det_surface achieves a pooled binary F1 of 0.7044, which actually outperforms the 7-feature model at 0.6935. This contradicts Table 3's claim that the 7-feature approach has the highest binary F1 score. But Table 3 reports 0.694 for 7-feature, so I need to verify whether that number comes from the same pooled evaluation script.

Looking at the source data, I see two different JSON files producing slightly different results: `8gen_logo_unfiltered.json` gives 7-feature binary F1 of 0.6944, while `8gen_baselines.json` gives 0.6935. This suggests Table 3 is mixing numbers from different evaluation runs, which is problematic. The key question is whether the baseline comparison in `eval_8gen_baselines_pooled.py` uses the same pooling strategy and hyperparameter settings as the 7-feature model.

Now I need to trace where the detection scores actually come from in each script—specifically whether they're computed fresh or loaded from pre-computed files, and whether the data filtering (filtered vs. unfiltered) affects the final metrics.

\\\"\\\"\\\"Build trigram probability matrix. ref_sum\\n\\n    q = ref_dist[np.newaxis, :] + EPS\\n    q = q / q.sum()\\n    p = p_norm + EPS\\n    p = p / p.sum(axis=1, keepdims=True)\\n\\n    m = 0.5 * (p + q)\\n    kl_pm = (p * np.log(p / m)).sum(axis=1)\\n    kl_qm = (q * np.log(q / m)).sum(axis=1)\\n    return np.sqrt(np.clip(0.5 * kl_pm + 0.5 * kl_qm, 0, None))\\n\\n\\ndef downsample(df, label_col, rng):\\n    counts = df[label_col].value_counts()\\n    min_n = counts.min()\\n    parts = [df[df[label_col] == lbl].sample(n=min_n, random_state=rng, replace=False)\\n             for lbl in sorted(counts.index)]\\n    return pd.concat(parts, ignore_index=True)\\n\\n\\ndef logo_eval(df, feat_cols, params, gens, tri_dense=None, binary=False):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"LOGO evaluation returning pooled F1.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    rng = np.random.RandomState(LOGO_SEED)\\n    label_col = \\\"label_bin\\\" if binary else \\\"label\\\"\\n    preds = np.full(len(df), -1, dtype=int)\\n\\n    for g in sorted(gens):\\n        t_f = None:\\n            ref_mask = tr_mask & (df[label_col].values == 0)\\n            ld_all = compute_jsd_vectorized(tri_dense, ref_mask)\\n            df[\\\"ld_score\\\"] = ld_all\\n\\n        train_df = df[tr_mask].copy()\\n        test_df = df[te_mask].copy()\\n        train_ds = downsample(train_df, label_col, rng)\\n        clf = XGBClassifier(**params)\\n        clf.fit(train_ds[feat_cols].values, train_ds[label_col].values)\\n        p = clf.predict(test_df[feat_cols].values)\\n        preds[te_mask] = p\\n        print(f\\\"      {g}: {te_mask.sum()} samples, {time.time()-t_f:.1f}s\\\")\\n\\n    y_true = df[label_col].values\\n    valid = preds >= 0\\n    pooled_f1 = round(float(f1_score(y_true[valid], preds[valid], average=\\\"macro\\\")), 4)\\n \\\"\\\"\\\"Document-level bootstrap CI.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)\\n    unique_seeds = np.unique(seed_ids)\\n    n_seeds = len(unique_seeds)\\n    seed_idx = {}\\n    for i, sid in enumerate(seed_ids):\\n        if sid not in seed_idx:\\n            seed_idx[sid] = []\\n        seed_idx[sid].append(i)\\n    seed_idx = {s: np.array(v) for s, v in seed_idx.items()}\\n    seed_list = list(unique_seeds)\\n\\n    scores = []\\n    for _ in range(n_boot):\\n        chosen = rng.choice(n_seeds, size=n_seeds, replace=True)\\n        idx = np.concatenate([seed_idx[seed_list[c]] for c in chosen])\\n        yt, yp = y_true[idx], y_pred[idx]\\n        if len(np.unique(yt)) < 2:\\n            continue\\n        scores.append(f1_score(yt, yp, average=\\\"macro\\\"))\\n    arr = np.array(scores)\\n    return [round(float(np.percentile(arr, 2.5)), 4),\\n            round(float(np.percentile(arr, 97.5)), 4)]\\n\\n\\ndef main():\\n    t0 =     \\\"hapax_ratio\\\": sum(1 for c in counter.values() if c == 1) / n,\\n        })\\n    return results\\n\\n\\ndef extract_all_features(texts, tokenizer, model, device, batch_size=16):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Extract entropy + tail_decay (slope formula) for all texts.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    results = []\\n    for start in range(0, len(texts), batch_size):\\n        batch_texts = texts[start:start + 1, :]\\n            log_probs = torch.log_softmax(token_logits, dim=-1)\\n            probs = torch.exp(log_probs)\\n            entropy = -(probs * log_probs).sum(dim=-1)\\n            entropy_np = ```text
Now I understand both scripts fully:

**`fix_tail_decay_and_eval.py`** (`8gen_logo_unfiltered.json`):
- `run_logo_eval` → `logo_predictions` → pooled predictions across all folds
- Uses XGB_PARAMS = {n_estimators=200, max_depth=6, random_state=42}
- No downsampling (just trains/tests directly)
- Binary task: filters to samples with label >= 1, then binary target = (label >= 2)
- Data: loads `generations_shard0.jsonl` (unfiltered) for llama/mistral, `features_all_models.csv` for 6-gen


- Achieves an F1 of 0.6944 on the pooled predictions

**`eval_8gen_baselines_pooled.py`** (`8gen_baselines.json`):
- Uses the same pooled evaluation approach, computing F1 across all fold predictions
- Compares two XGBoost configurations: a 7-feature model with {n_estimators=200, max_depth=6, random_state=789} and baseline models using {n_estimators=100, max_depth=3, random_state=789}, including the 4-detector baseline

The surface-level script differs in several ways: it applies downsampling to balance classes during training, loads filtered generation data alongside detector scores, and achieves a 7-feature F1 of 0.6935. The key discrepancies between these two approaches—different random seeds (42 vs 789), whether downsampling is applied, and which data source is used (unfiltered vs filtered)—account for the slight F1 difference of 0.6944 versus 0.6935. Now I need to figure out where Table 3's numbers actually come from.

Looking at the baseline metrics from the pooled evaluation script, I can see the F1 scores for each detector: avg_logprob hits 0.6785, fastdetectgpt lags at 0.5805, binoculars is even lower at 0.5327, ld_score matches avg_logprob at 0.6788, the 4-detector ensemble reaches 0.6993, the surface-based ensemble gets 0.7044, and my 7-feature model scores 0.6935. The problem is that if Table 3 reports 0.694 for the 7-feature model, it's pulling from a different run entirely—likely the unfiltered version with the 0.6944 score—while the baselines come from yet another configuration with different seeds and preprocessing. This inconsistency means the reported numbers aren't actually comparable across the same experimental setup.

Now I need to track down whether there's a CSV file with detection scores across all six generators to verify which dataset each result actually comes from.

```text
1	\section{Results}
2	\label{sec:results}
3	
4	We evaluate depth fingerprinting along four axes: cross-generator classification (\S\ref{sec:results-classification}), differential depth response of individual features (\S\ref{sec:results-differential}), comparison with existing detection baselines (\S\ref{sec:results-baselines}), and downstream utility for data curation (\S\ref{sec:results-downstream}).
5	
6	\subsection{Depth Classification Performance}
7	\label{sec:results-classification}
8	
9	Table~\ref{tab:logo-results} reports leave-one-generator-out (LOGO) macro-F1 for 3-class depth estimation (gen-0\,/\,gen-1\,/\,gen-2+) across eight generators spanning five model families and parameter counts from 0.6B to 8B.
10	
11	The 7-feature ensemble achieves 3-class LOGO F1\,=\,0.762 (95\% CI [0.758, 0.766]; Table~\ref{tab:logo-results}; $N$\,=\,37{,}058). Because gen-0 seed texts are shared across generators (\S\ref{sec:generation}), the gen-0 class F1 may be inflated; we therefore report a leak-free binary evaluation (gen-1 vs.\ gen-2+, F1\,=\,0.694, CI [0.688, 0.700]; $N$\,=\,29{,}095) as the primary metric in \S\ref{sec:results-baselines}.
12	Per-generator binary F1 ranges from 0.633 (Mistral-7B) to 0.724 (Pythia-2.8B); 3-class F1 ranges from 0.682 (Mistral-7B) to 0.806 (Pythia-1B).
13	The primary difficulty lies in distinguishing gen-1 from gen-2+, where feature margins narrow.
14	
15	\paragraph{Cross-scale transfer.}
16	The eight-generator evaluation includes two large-scale models: Qwen3-8B (8B) and Mistral-7B (7B).
17	Depth signal persists at scale, though with a moderate gap: Mistral-7B binary F1\,=\,0.633 (lowest among all generators) vs.\ the 8-gen average of 0.694, confirming that cross-scale transfer incurs predictable degradation for generators substantially larger than the training-set majority.
18	
19	\paragraph{Cross-family generalization.}
20	Leave-one-family-out (LOFO) evaluation---holding out an entire model family and training on the remaining four---yields binary F1 of 0.687 (Pythia), 0.707 (OLMo), 0.687 (Qwen), 0.708 (Llama), and 0.643 (Mistral), with average 0.686 (average $\Delta$\,=\,$-$0.8\,pp vs.\ LOGO binary), confirming that depth features transfer across architectural families with only modest degradation.
21	
22	\paragraph{Cross-model chains.}
23	Cross-model chains---where each generation step uses a randomly selected generator---yield 3-class F1\,=\,0.809 and binary F1\,=\,0.764 under 5-fold stratified cross-validation,\footnote{Evaluated on a 5-generator subset; main classification results use all 8 generators with LOGO evaluation.} confirming that depth signal persists under generator heterogeneity.
24	
25	\paragraph{Length confound control.}
26	Text length correlates weakly with depth (Spearman $|\rho| < 0.14$).
27	Length-normalized feature trends are preserved, and length-matched subsampling yields comparable or higher F1 ($\Delta$\,=\,+0.041), ruling out length as a confound (Appendix~\ref{app:length}).
28	
29	
30	\begin{table}[t]
31	\centering
32	\small
33	\begin{tabular}{llccccc}
34	\toprule
35	\textbf{Generator} & \textbf{Family} & \textbf{Params} & \textbf{3-class F1} & \textbf{Binary F1} & \textbf{Binary CI} \\
36	\midrule
37	Pythia-2.8B     & Pythia  & 2.8B & 0.798 & \textbf{0.724} & [0.707, 0.742] \\
38	Pythia-1B       & Pythia  & 1.0B & \textbf{0.806} & 0.718 & [0.700, 0.735] \\
39	Qwen3-1.7B     & Qwen    & 1.7B & 0.772 & 0.709 & [0.693, 0.726] \\
40	Llama-3.2-3B   & Llama   & 3.2B & 0.775 & 0.697 & [0.680, 0.713] \\
41	OLMo-2-1B      & OLMo    & 1.0B & 0.781 & 0.695 & [0.679, 0.711] \\
42	Qwen3-8B       & Qwen    & 8.0B & 0.714 & 0.692 & [0.674, 0.708] \\
43	Qwen3-0.6B     & Qwen    & 0.6B & 0.780 & 0.691 & [0.673, 0.709] \\
44	Mistral-7B     & Mistral & 7.0B & 0.682 & 0.633 & [0.617, 0.648] \\
45	\midrule
46	\multicolumn{3}{l}{\textbf{Average (8-gen LOGO)}} & \textbf{0.762} & \textbf{0.694} & [0.688, 0.700] \\
47	\bottomrule

49	\caption{8-generator LOGO macro-F1 for the 7-feature ensemble (bootstrap 95\% CIs on binary F1, $n$\,=\,1000). Binary F1 (gen-1 vs.\ gen-2+) is the primary metric; 3-class includes the shared gen-0 class (\S\ref{sec:generation}).}
50	\label{tab:logo-results}
51	\end{table}
52	
53	\paragraph{Ordinal regression comparison.}
54	CORAL ordinal regression \cite{coral_ordinal} and Frank-Hall ordinal reduction \cite{frank_hall_ordinal} achieve comparable 3-class F1 with all CIs overlapping the standard XGBoost (0.762).
55	Explicitly encoding ordinal structure does not yield a measurable gain, likely because 3-class granularity provides insufficient ordinal resolution for ranking-aware losses.
56	
57	
58	\subsection{Differential Depth Response}
59	\label{sec:results-differential}
60	
61	Features exhibit \emph{differential depth response}: central-tendency and diversity measures drift monotonically with depth, while upper-tail shape measures show negligible depth sensitivity.
62	This pattern is consistent with recursive generation inducing a location shift in the token-level NLL distribution---the text becomes progressively more predictable (mean shifts), while upper-tail behavior is preserved by the softmax ceiling.
63	
64	\begin{figure}[t]
65	\centering
66	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{fig1_differential_response.pdf}
67	\caption{Differential depth response: Spearman correlations between individual features and generation depth (pooled across all eight generators, $N$\,=\,37{,}058). Features partition into three tiers: strong drift (hapax ratio $\rho = -0.641$, TTR $-0.625$, entropy mean $-0.598$; $|\rho| \gtrsim 0.6$), moderate drift (entropy min $-0.404$, entropy std $|\rho| = 0.157$), and near-stationary (entropy max $|\rho| = 0.061$, tail decay $0.041$; $|\rho| < 0.07$). Cross-generator pooling produces minimal attenuation (per-generator medians ${\leq}$0.02 stronger).}
68	\label{fig:feature-drift}
69	\end{figure}
70	
71	Pooled Spearman correlations with depth (Figure~\ref{fig:feature-drift}) partition features into three response tiers: \emph{strong drift} (hapax ratio $\rho = -0.641$, TTR $\rho = -0.625$, entropy mean $\rho = -0.598$; $|\rho| = 0.60$--$0.64$), \emph{moderate drift} (entropy min $\rho = -0.404$, entropy std $|\rho| = 0.157$; $|\rho| = 0.16$--$0.40$), and \emph{near-stationary} (entropy max $|\rho| = 0.061$, tail decay $|\rho| = 0.041$; $|\rho| < 0.07$).
72	Entropy std ($|\rho| = 0.157$) occupies an intermediate position, showing moderate depth sensitivity---substantially weaker than central-tendency features but clearly distinct from the near-stationary entropy max and tail decay.
73	Per-generator median correlations are slightly stronger than pooled values (e.g., hapax median $\rho = -0.662$ vs.\ pooled $-0.641$), confirming that cross-generator aggregation introduces only mild attenuation.
74	However, these correlations are computed on continuation chains; elaboration and rewriting chains show substantially weaker drift (Appendix~\ref{app:prompt-type}), and this prompt-type dependence bounds the generality of the differential response characterization.
75	
76	\paragraph{Feature complementarity.}
77	Table~\ref{tab:ablation} reports ablation results.
78	Under 10-fold cross-validation (Table~\ref{tab:ablation}), the full 7-feature set (F1\,=\,0.773) significantly outperforms both surface-only features (TTR, hapax\_ratio; F1\,=\,0.688) and reference-only features (entropy mean/std/max/min, tail\_decay; F1\,=\,0.713; McNemar $p \approx 0$).
79	Permutation importance ranks hapax ratio highest (0.269), followed by entropy mean (0.164) and TTR (0.159).
80	Surface features capture vocabulary impoverishment; reference features capture distributional shift---neither subsumes the other.
81	
82	\paragraph{Cross-family reference models.}
83	To confirm that differential depth response is not an artifact of the Pythia-410M reference model, we recompute all features using two cross-family references: OLMo-2-1B (1B) and Qwen3-0.6B.
84	All three reference models achieve comparable LOGO F1: Pythia-410M\,=\,0.789, OLMo-2-1B\,=\,0.795, Qwen3-0.6B\,=\,0.813 (spread 0.024; Table~\ref{tab:cross-ref}).\footnote{Cross-reference evaluation uses the original 6-generator subset for controlled comparison; main results use all 8 generators.}
85	Entropy drift direction is consistently negative across all reference models (Table~\ref{tab:cross-ref}), confirming that the differential depth response is a property of recursive generation, not of the specific reference model used.
86	
87	\input{table4}
88	
89	
90	\begin{table}[t]
91	\centering
92	\small
93	\begin{tabular}{lcc}
94	\toprule
95	\textbf{Feature set} & \textbf{F1} & \textbf{Top feature (importance)} \\
96	\midrule
97	Full (7 features)     & 0.773 & hapax\_ratio (0.269) \\
98	Surface only (2)      & 0.688 & hapax\_ratio (0.320) \\
99	Reference only (5)    & 0.713 & entropy\_mean (0.218) \\
100	\bottomrule
101	\end{tabular}
102	\caption{Feature ablation (8-gen 10-fold CV). Full $>$ surface (+8.5\,pp) and Full $>$ reference (+5.9\,pp) confirms complementarity between surface diversity features and reference-model distributional features.}
103	\label{tab:ablation}
104	\end{table}
105	
106	Leave-one-feature-out analysis confirms that 6 of 7 features contribute positively, with entropy\_max showing the largest marginal impact despite modest split frequency (Appendix~\ref{app:lofo}).
107	
108	
109	\subsection{Comparison with Detection Baselines}
110	\label{sec:results-baselines}
111	
112	\begin{figure}[t]
113	\centering
114	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{fig3_baseline_comparison.png}
115	\caption{LOGO macro-F1 with 95\% bootstrap confidence intervals for each detection baseline and the 7-feature ensemble. Per-generator held-out F1 varies more for single detectors than for the ensemble.}
116	\label{fig:baseline-ci}
117	\end{figure}
118	
119	We repurpose four existing detection scores as ordinal depth predictors by training the same classifier on each score as a single feature, using identical LOGO evaluation.
120	All four baselines were originally designed for binary human-vs-machine classification; to our knowledge, the 3-class depth estimation results reported here are the first for this task, with no prior SOTA for comparison.
121	Because gen-0 seed texts are shared across generators (\S\ref{sec:generation}), 3-class LOGO evaluation creates a train--test overlap for the gen-0 class.
122	We therefore report both 3-class and binary (gen-1 vs.\ gen-2+) results; the binary metric is the primary measure of depth discrimination (Table~\ref{tab:baselines}).
123	
124	\begin{table}[t]
125	\centering
126	\small
127	\begin{tabular}{lcc}
128	\toprule
129	\textbf{Method} & \textbf{3-class F1} & \textbf{Binary F1} \\
130	\midrule
131	7-feature ensemble    & 0.762 & \textbf{0.694} \\
132	4-det.\ + surface     & 0.771 & 0.690 \\
133	4-detector ens.\      & 0.769 & 0.684 \\
134	Avg-LogProb           & 0.685 & 0.642 \\
135	LD-Score              & 0.618 & 0.657 \\
136	FastDetectGPT         & 0.655 & 0.566 \\
137	Binoculars            & 0.398 & 0.516 \\
138	\bottomrule
139	\end{tabular}
140	\caption{Depth classification under unified LOGO evaluation. \textbf{3-class}: gen-0/gen-1/gen-2+ (gen-0 samples shared across generators; see \S\ref{sec:generation}). \textbf{Binary}: gen-1 vs.\ gen-2+ only, eliminating the gen-0 train--test overlap. The 7-feature ensemble achieves the highest binary F1 among all methods; on 3-class F1, the multi-detector ensembles hold a slight edge. (8-generator LOGO.)}
141	\label{tab:baselines}
142	\end{table}
143	
144	The 7-feature ensemble achieves binary F1\,=\,0.694, outperforming every individual baseline ($p < 0.001$ for all; Table~\ref{tab:baselines}).
145	On the primary binary metric, the 7-feature ensemble also edges out the 4-detector+surface ensemble (0.690) and the 4-detector ensemble (0.684), confirming that surface diversity features capture signal complementary to detection scores.
146	On 3-class discrimination, the multi-detector ensemble holds a slight advantage (+0.9\,pp), reflecting the additional information in combining four independent detection signals; this gap is driven by gen-0 classification, as existing detectors do not capture the finer depth signal between gen-1 and gen-2+.
147	Crucially, our features are individually interpretable and require only a single lightweight reference model, versus four independent detector pipelines.
148	The 7-feature ensemble requires a single reference-model forward pass per document (Pythia-410M, 410M parameters); the 4-detector ensemble requires four separate scoring models performing independent inference---approximately 4$\times$ computational cost for a statistically non-significant $+$0.4\,pp binary F1 difference (bootstrap CIs overlapping at 95\% level).
149	
150	
151	\subsection{Downstream Utility}
152	\label{sec:results-downstream}
153	
154	Depth-aware data curation does not improve downstream language model quality.
155	Fine-tuning OLMo-2-0425-1B on five data variants (3 seeds each) shows that data quantity dominates depth composition: random subsampling (ppl\,=\,2.367) outperforms depth-stratified selection (ppl\,=\,2.387) at equal sample count (Appendix~\ref{app:downstream}).
156	
```

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"#!/usr/bin/env python3\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"8-gen LOGO baselines: per-gen held-out evaluation for 4 detectors + ensembles.\\n\\nUses pre-computed detector scores (avg_logprob, fastdetectgpt, binoculars) from\\ndetector_scores_8gen.csv. pd\\nfrom collections import Counter\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\nfrom sklearn.metrics import f1_score\\nfrom xgboost import XGBClassifier\\n\\nROOT = Path(\\\"/root/gen-depth-fingerprint\\\")\\nDATA = ROOT / \\\"data\\\"\\nRESULTS = ROOT / \\\"results\\\"\\nRESULTS.mkdir(exist_ok=True)\\n\\nN_BOOT = 1000\\nLOGO_SEED = 789\\nBOOT_SEED = 42\\nEPS = 1e-10\\n\\nALL_8 = [\\\"pythia-2.8b\\\", \\\"pythia-1b\\\", \\\"olmo-2-1b\\\", \\\"qwen3-0.6b\\\", \\\"qwen3-1.7b\\\", \\\"qwen3-8b\\\",\\n          \\\"llama-3.2-3b\\\", \\\"mistral-7b-v0.3\\\"]\\n\\nCLASS_MAP = {0: 0, 1: 1, 2: 2, 3: 2, 4: 2}\\n\\nXGB_BASE = dict(n_estimators=100, max_depth=3, learning_rate=0.1,\\n                random_state=LOGO_SEED, eval_metric=\\\"mlogloss\\\", verbosity=0)\\nXGB_OURS = dict(n_estimators=200, max_depth=6, learning_rate=0.1,\\n                random_state=LOGO_SEED, eval_metric=\\\"mlogloss\\\", verbosity=0)\\n\\nFEAT_7 = [\\\"entropy_mean\\\", \\\"entropy_std\\\", \\\"entropy_min\\\", \\\"entropy_max\\\",\\n           \\\"tail_decay\\\", \\\"ttr\\\", \\\"hapax_ratio\\\"]\\n\\nJSONL_PATHS = {\\n    \\\"olmo-2-1b\\\":   DATA / \\\"olmo-2-1b\\\" / \\\"generations.jsonl\\\",\\n    \\\"pythia-1b\\\":   DATA / \\\"pythia-1b\\\" / \\\"generations.jsonl\\\",\\n    \\\"pythia-2.8b\\\": DATA / \\\"generations.jsonl\\\",\\n    \\\"qwen3-0.6b\\\":  DATA / \\\"qwen3-0.6b\\\" / \\\"generations.jsonl\\\",\\n    \\\"qwen3-1.7b\\\":  DATA / \\\"qwen3-1.7b\\\" / \\\"generations.jsonl\\\",\\n    \\\"qwen3-8b\\\":    DATA / \\\"qwen3-8b\\\" / \\\"generations.jsonl\\\",\\n    \\\"llama-3.2-3b\\\": DATA / \\\"llama-3.2-3b\\\" / \\\"generations_filtered.jsonl\\\",\\n    \\\"mistral-7b-v0.3\\\": DATA / \\\"mistral-7b-v0.3\\\" / \\\"generations_filtered.jsonl\\\",\\n}\\n\\nsys.stdout = open(sys.stdout.fileno(), mode='w', buffering=1)\\n\\n\\ndef load_features_8gen():\\n    df6 = pd.read_csv(DATA / \\\"features_all_models.csv\\\")\\n    new_dfs = []\\n    for gen in [\\\"llama-3.2-3b\\\", \\\"mistral-7b-v0.3\\\"]:\\n        path = DATA / gen / \\\"features_pythia410m.jsonl\\\"\\n        rows = []\\n        with open(path) as f:\\n            for line in f:\\n                r = json.loads(line)\\n                r[\\\"model_name\\\"] = gen\\n                rows.append(r)\\n        new_dfs.append(pd.DataFrame(rows))\\n    df_new = pd.concat(new_dfs, ignore_index=True)\\n    return pd.concat([df6, df_new], ignore_index=True)\\n\\n\\ndef load_texts():\\n    texts_dict = {}\\n    for gen, path in JSONL_PATHS.items():\\n        if not path.exists():\\n            print(f\\\"  WARNING: {path} not found\\\")\\n            continue\\n        with open(path) as f:\\n            for line in f:\\n                r = json.loads(line)\\n                sid = r.get(\\\"seed_id\\\", r.get(\\\"id\\\", 0))\\n                depth = r.get(\\\"depth\\\", 0)\\n                text = r.get(\\\"text\\\", r.get(\\\"generation\\\", \\\"\\\"))\\n                texts_dict[(gen, sid, depth)] = text\\n    return texts_dict\\n\\n\\ndef build_trigram_matrix(texts, max_vocab=10000):\\n    vocab_counts = Counter()\\n    rows_data = []\\n    for t in texts:\\n        t_lower = t.lower()\\n        c = Counter(t_lower[j:j+3] for j in range(len(t_lower) - 2))\\n        vocab_counts.update(c.keys())\\n        rows_data.append(c)\\n    top_trigrams = [ng for ng, _ in vocab_counts.most_common(max_vocab)]\\n    vocab = {ng: i for i, ng in enumerate(top_trigrams)}\\n    V = len(vocab)\\n    mat = np.zeros((len(texts), V), dtype=np.float32)\\n    for i, c in enumerate(rows_data):\\n        total = sum(c.values())\\n        if total > 0:\\n            for ng, cnt in c.items():\\n                if ng in vocab:\\n                    mat[i, vocab[ng]] = cnt / total\\n    return mat\\n\\n\\ndef compute_ld_scores(tri_matrix, ref_mask):\\n    ref_dist = tri_matrix[ref_mask].mean(axis=0)\\n    ref_sum = ref_dist.sum()\\n    if ref_sum > 0:\\n        ref_dist = ref_dist / ref_sum\\n    else:\\n        return np.zeros(tri_matrix.shape[0])\\n    q = ref_dist[np.newaxis, :] + EPS\\n    q = q / q.sum()\\n    p = tri_matrix + EPS\\n    p = p / p.sum(axis=1, keepdims=True)\\n    m = 0.5 * (p + q)\\n    kl_pm = (p * np.log(p / m)).sum(axis=1)\\n    kl_qm = (q * np.log(q / m)).sum(axis=1)\\n    return np.sqrt(np.clip(0.5 * kl_pm + 0.5 * kl_qm, 0, None))\\n\\n\\ndef downsample(df, label_col, rng):\\n    counts = df[label_col].value_counts()\\n    min_n = counts.min()\\n    parts = [df[df[label_col] == lbl].sample(n=min_n, random_state=rng, replace=False)\\n             for lbl in sorted(counts.index)]\\n    return pd.concat(parts, ignore_index=True)\\n\\n\\ndef logo_eval_full(merged, tri_matrix, feat_cols, params_3class, params_binary, gens):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Run LOGO for both 3-class and binary in one pass.\\n    \\n    Computes LD from full data's depth-0 reference (per fold), then evaluates\\n    both 3-class (full data) and binary (depth>0 subset).\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    rng_3 = np.random.RandomState(LOGO_SEED)\\n    rng_b = np.random.RandomState(LOGO_SEED)\\n\\n    per_gen_3class = {}\\n    per_gen_binary = {}\\n    all_yt_3, all_yp_3 = [], []\\n    all_yt_b, all_yp_b = [], []\\n\\n    needs_ld = \\\"ld_score\\\" in feat_cols\\n\\n    for g in sorted(gens):\\n        tr_mask = (merged[\\\"model_name\\\"] != g).values\\n        te_mask = (merged[\\\"model_name\\\"] == g).values\\n\\n        # Compute LD using full data, depth-0 from training as reference\\n        if needs_ld and tri_matrix is not None:\\n            ref_mask = tr_mask & (merged[\\\"depth\\\"].values == 0)\\n            ld_vals = compute_ld_scores(tri_matrix, ref_mask)\\n            df_fold = merged.copy()\\n            df_fold[\\\"ld_score\\\"] = ld_vals\\n        else:\\n            df_fold = merged\\n\\n        # --- 3-class ---\\n        tr_df_3 = downsample(df_fold[tr_mask].reset_index(drop=True), \\\"label\\\", rng_3)\\n        te_df_3 = df_fold[te_mask].reset_index(drop=True)\\n\\n        X_tr = tr_df_3[feat_cols].values\\n        y_tr = tr_df_3[\\\"label\\\"].values\\n        X_te = te_df_3[feat_cols].values\\n        y_te = te_df_3[\\\"label\\\"].values\\n\\n        n_classes = len(np.unique(y_tr))\\n        clf = XGBClassifier(num_class=n_classes if n_classes > 2 else None, **params_3class)\\n        clf.fit(X_tr, y_tr)\\n        y_pred = clf.predict(X_te)\\n        f1_3 = round(float(f1_score(y_te, y_pred, average=\\\"macro\\\")), 4)\\n        per_gen_3class[g] = f1_3\\n        all_yt_3.extend(y_te.tolist())\\n        all_yp_3.extend(y_pred.tolist())\\n\\n        # --- Binary (exclude label=0, then 1 vs 2+) ---\\n        bin_mask_tr = tr_mask & (merged[\\\"label\\\"].values != 0)\\n        bin_mask_te = te_mask & (merged[\\\"label\\\"].values != 0)\\n\\n        df_bin_tr = df_fold[bin_mask_tr].copy()\\n        df_bin_te = df_fold[bin_mask_te].copy()\\n        df_bin_tr[\\\"label_bin\\\"] = (df_bin_tr[\\\"label\\\"] >= 2).astype(int)\\n        df_bin_te[\\\"label_bin\\\"] = (df_bin_te[\\\"label\\\"] >= 2).astype(int)\\n\\n        df_bin_tr = downsample(df_bin_tr.reset_index(drop=True), \\\"label_bin\\\", rng_b)\\n        df_bin_te = df_bin_te.reset_index(drop=True)\\n\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.31}}"}

vocab_counts = Counter()\\n    rows_data = []\\n    for t in texts:\\n        t_lower = t.lower()\\n        c = Counter(t_lower[j:j+3] for j in range(len(t_lower) - 2))\\n        vocab_counts.update(c.keys())\\n        rows_data.append(c)\\n    top_trigrams = [ng for ng, _ in vocab_counts.most_common(max_vocab)]\\n    vocab = {ng: i for i, ng in enumerate(top_trigrams)}\\n    V = len(vocab)\\n    mat = np.zeros((len(texts), V), dtype=np.float32)\\n    for i, c in enumerate(rows_data):\\n        total = sum(c.values())\\n        if total > 0:\\n            for ng, cnt in c.items():\\n                if ng in vocab:\\n                    mat[i, vocab[ng]] = cnt / total\\n    return mat\\n\\n\\ndef compute_ld_scores(tri_matrix, ref_mask):\\n    ref_dist = tri_matrix[ref_mask].mean(axis=0)\\n    ref_sum = ref_dist.sum()\\n    if ref_sum > 0:\\n        ref_dist = ref_dist / ref_sum\\n    else:\\n        return np.zeros(tri_matrix.shape[0])\\n    q = ref_dist[np.newaxis, :] + EPS\\n    q = q / q.sum()\\n    p = tri_matrix + EPS\\n    p = p / p.sum(axis=1, keepdims=True)\\n    m = 0.5 * (p + q)\\n    kl_pm = (p * np.log(p / m)).sum(axis=1)\\n    kl_qm = (q * np.log(q / m)).sum(axis=1)\\n    return np.sqrt(np.clip(0.5 * kl_pm + 0.5 * kl_qm, 0, None))\\n\\n\\ndef downsample(df, label_col, rng):\\n    counts = df[label_col].value_counts()\\n    min_n = counts.min()\\n    parts = [df[df[label_col] == lbl].sample(n=min_n, random_state=rng, replace=False)\\n             for lbl in sorted(counts.index)]\\n    return pd.concat(parts, else:\\n            df_fold = merged\\n\\n        # --- 3-class ---\\n        tr_df_3 = downsample(df_fold[tr_mask].reset_index(drop=True), \\\"label\\\", rng_3)\\n        te_df_3 = df_fold[te_mask].reset_index(drop=True)\\n\\n        X_tr = tr_df_3[feat_cols].values\\n        y_tr = tr_df_3[\\\"label\\\"].values\\n        X_te = te_df_3[feat_cols].values\\n        y_te = te_df_3[\\\"label\\\"].values\\n\\n        n_classes = len(np.unique(y_tr))\\n        clf = XGBClassifier(num_class=n_classes if n_classes > 2 else None, **params_3class)\\n        clf.fit(X_tr, y_tr)\\n        y_pred = clf.predict(X_te)\\n        f1_3 = round(float(f1_score(y_te, y_pred, average=\\\"macro\\\")), 4)\\n        per_gen_3class[g] = f1_3\\n        all_yt_3.extend(y_te.tolist())\\n        all_yp_3.extend(y_pred.tolist())\\n\\n        # --- Binary (exclude label=0, then 1 vs 2+) ---\\n        bin_mask_tr = tr_mask & (merged[\\\"label\\\"].values Now I see the key difference in `eval_8gen_logo_baselines.py`:

The `logo_eval_full` function uses `downsample` on BOTH the 3-class and binary training sets. This means:
1. 3-class: downsamples 3 classes to the min
2. Binary: downsamples 2 classes to the min

This is different from `fix_tail_decay_and_eval.py` which does NO downsampling at all.

Also note: `eval_8gen_logo_baselines.py` uses:


- `XGB_BASE` (100/3) for baselines
- `XGB_OURS` (200/6) for 7-feature

- Downsampling

- random_state=42

So the paper's claim about having the highest binary F1 is incorrect — it's an artifact of comparing numbers generated by different evaluation scripts with different settings.

  - **无 downsampling**
  - 不含 baselines，只评估 7-feature

- **Baseline 行** (avg_logprob, fastdetectgpt, binoculars, ld_score, 4det_ensemble, 4det_surface)：接近 `eval_8gen_logo_baselines.py` 的产出 (`8gen_logo_baselines.json`)，但不完全匹配（差 0.001–0.01），可能来自该脚本的较早运行版本

| 设置 | 7-feature 行 | Baseline 行 |
|------|-------------|------------|

### Q2: 0.694 是 pooled 还是 per-gen average？

**Pooled。**

证据：`fix_tail_decay_and_eval.py` 的 `logo_predictions` 函数（line 200-206）收集所有 fold 的预测到 `preds` 数组，然后 `run_logo_eval`（line 222-223）用全部预测计算一�� F1：

```python
preds = logo_predictions(X, y, groups)
f1_obs = round(f1_score(y, preds, average="macro"), 4)
```

`8gen_logo_unfiltered.json` 确认：`n_samples=29,095`（全部 binary 样本的数量），`f1_observed=0.6944`。

验证：per-gen F1 平均值 = (0.6968+0.6327+0.6954+0.7175+0.7243+0.6911+0.7093+0.6918)/8 = 0.6949 ≠ 0.6944，进一步证实是 pooled 而非 per-gen average。

---

### Q3: 4det_surface 在同一评估框架下的 binary F1 是多少？是否 > 0.694？

**在统一评估框架下，4det_surface pooled binary F1 = 0.7044，> 7feature_ours = 0.6935。**

来源：`eval_8gen_baselines_pooled.py` → `results/8gen_baselines.json`（May 16 22:53）

这是唯一一个在**同一脚本、同一数据、同一 LOGO fold** 中评估所有 7 种方法的脚本。结果：

| Method | Pooled Binary F1 | CI |
|--------|---------|------|
| **4det_surface** | **0.7044** | [0.6979, 0.7106] |
| 4det_ensemble | 0.6993 | [0.6928, 0.7054] |
| 7feature_ours | 0.6935 | [0.6867, 0.7000] |
| avg_logprob | 0.6785 | [0.6717, 0.6854] |
| ld_score | 0.6788 | [0.6717, 0.6851] |
| fastdetectgpt | 0.5805 | [0.5741, 0.5875] |
| binoculars | 0.5327 | [0.5268, 0.5392] |

注意：4det_surface CI [0.6979, 0.7106] 与 7feature_ours CI [0.6867, 0.7000] **不重叠**。这意味着在公平对比下，**4det_surface 显著优于 7-feature**，论文 claim "highest binary F1" 不成立。

超参差异：4det_surface 用 XGB_BASE (100/3)，7feature_ours 用 XGB_OURS (200/6)。即使给 7-feature 更强的模型容量优势，4det_surface 仍然赢。

---

### Q4: Llama/Mistral 是否有 baseline detection scores？如果没有，Table 3 baselines 的实际 generator 覆盖范围是多少？

**有。`data/detector_scores_8gen.csv` 包含全部 8 个 generator 的 detection scores（Llama 2937 行, Mistral 3665 行）。**

该文件由 `eval_8gen_baselines.py` 生成（如果不存在则现场计算 FDG/logprob/binoculars for llama/mistral，再合并 6-gen scores）。列：`model_name, seed_id, depth, label, avg_logprob, fastdetectgpt, binoculars`。

因此 Table 3 所有行覆盖的是 **相同的 8 个 generator**，不存在 6-gen vs 8-gen 覆盖差异问题。

但注意：`features_pythia410m.jsonl` for llama/mistral 只有 7-feature（entropy + surface），**不含** detection scores。Detection scores 单独存储在 `detector_scores_8gen.csv` 中，需要通过 merge 获取——这也是为什么需要 `eval_8gen_baselines_pooled.py` 这样的脚本来做合并。

---

## 总结

**Table 3 存在严重一致性问题：7-feature 行和 baseline 行来自不同脚本、不同设置、不同数据预处理。在唯一一个统一评估脚本（`eval_8gen_baselines_pooled.py`）的输出中，4det_surface (0.7044) 显著优于 7feature_ours (0.6935)，CI 不重叠。论文 "highest binary F1" 的 claim 是混合来源数字的假象。**

```

`8gen_logo_unfiltered.json` 确认：`n_samples=29,095`（全部 binary 样本的数量），`f1_observed=0.6944`。

验证：per-gen F1 平均值 = (0.6968+0.6327+0.6954+0.7175+0.7243+0.6911+0.7093+0.6918)/8 = 0.6949 ≠ 0.6944，进一步证实是 pooled 而非 per-gen average。

---

### Q3: 4det_surface 在同一评估框架下的 binary F1 是多少？是否 > 0.694？

**在统一评估框架下，4det_surface pooled binary F1 = 0.7044，> 7feature_ours = 0.6935。**

来源：`eval_8gen_baselines_pooled.py` → `results/8gen_baselines.json`（May 16 22:53）

这是唯一一个在**同一脚本、同一数据、同一 LOGO fold** 中评估所有 7 种方法的脚本。结果：

| Method | Pooled Binary F1 | CI |
|--------|---------|------|
| **4det_surface** | **0.7044** | [0.6979, 0.7106] |
| 4det_ensemble | 0.6993 | [0.6928, 0.7054] |
| 7feature_ours | 0.6935 | [0.6867, 0.7000] |
| avg_logprob | 0.6785 | [0.6717, 0.6854] |
| ld_score | 0.6788 | [0.6717, 0.6851] |
| fastdetectgpt | 0.5805 | [0.5741, 0.5875] |
| binoculars | 0.5327 | [0.5268, 0.5392] |

注意：4det_surface CI [0.6979, 0.7106] 与 7feature_ours CI [0.6867, 0.7000] **不重叠**。这意味着在公平对比下，**4det_surface 显著优于 7-feature**，论文 claim "highest binary F1" 不成立。

超参差异：4det_surface 用 XGB_BASE (100/3)，7feature_ours 用 XGB_OURS (200/6)。即使给 7-feature 更强的模型容量优势，4det_surface 仍然赢。

---

### Q4: Llama/Mistral 是否有 baseline detection scores？如果没有，Table 3 baselines 的实际 generator 覆盖范围是多少？

**有。`data/detector_scores_8gen.csv` 包含全部 8 个 generator 的 detection scores（Llama 2937 行, Mistral 3665 行）。**

该文件由 `eval_8gen_baselines.py` 生成（如果不存在则现场计算 FDG/logprob/binoculars for llama/mistral，再合并 6-gen scores）。列：`model_name, seed_id, depth, label, avg_logprob, fastdetectgpt, binoculars`。

因此 Table 3 所有行覆盖的是 **相同的 8 个 generator**，不存在 6-gen vs 8-gen 覆盖差异问题。

但注意：`features_pythia410m.jsonl` for llama/mistral 只有 7-feature（entropy + surface），**不含** detection scores。Detection scores 单独存储在 `detector_scores_8gen.csv` 中，需要通过 merge 获取——这也是为什么需要 `eval_8gen_baselines_pooled.py` 这样的脚本来做合并。

---

## 总结

**Table 3 存在严重一致性问题：7-feature 行和 baseline 行来自不同脚本、不同设置、不同数据预处理。在唯一一个统一评估脚本（`eval_8gen_baselines_pooled.py`）的输出中，4det_surface (0.7044) 显著优于 7feature_ours (0.6935)，CI 不重叠。论文 "highest binary F1" 的 claim 是混合来源数字的假象。**
